=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
The manufacturing of a certain product requires N processes numbered 1,2,\dots,N.
For each process i, there are two types of machines S_i and T_i available for purchase to handle it.

- Machine S_i: Can process A_i products per day per unit, and costs P_i yen per unit.
- Machine T_i: Can process B_i products per day per unit, and costs Q_i yen per unit.

You can purchase any number of each machine, possibly zero.
Suppose that process i can handle W_i products per day as a result of introducing machines.
Here, we define the production capacity as the minimum of W, that is, \displaystyle \min^{N}_{i=1} W_i.
Given a total budget of X yen, find the maximum achievable production capacity.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N X
A_1 P_1 B_1 Q_1
A_2 P_2 B_2 Q_2
\vdots
A_N P_N B_N Q_N

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- All input values are integers.
- 1 \le N \le 100
- 1 \le A_i,B_i \le 100
- 1 \le P_i,Q_i,X \le 10^7

Sample Input 1

3 22
2 5 3 6
1 1 3 3
1 3 2 4

Sample Output 1

4

For example, by introducing machines as follows, we can achieve a production capacity of 4, which is the maximum possible.

- For process 1, introduce 2 units of machine S_1.
- This allows processing 4 products per day and costs a total of 10 yen.


- For process 2, introduce 1 unit of machine S_2.
- This allows processing 1 product per day and costs a total of 1 yen.


- For process 2, introduce 1 unit of machine T_2.
- This allows processing 3 products per day and costs a total of 3 yen.


- For process 3, introduce 2 units of machine T_3.
- This allows processing 4 products per day and costs a total of 8 yen.

Sample Input 2

1 10000000
100 1 100 1

Sample Output 2

1000000000

Sample Input 3

1 1
1 10000000 1 10000000

Sample Output 3

0

There may be cases where a positive production capacity cannot be achieved.

Sample Input 4

10 7654321
8 6 9 1
5 6 4 3
2 4 7 9
7 8 9 1
7 9 1 6
4 8 9 1
2 2 8 9
1 6 2 6
4 2 3 4
6 6 5 2

Sample Output 4

894742

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's define min_cost(C, A,P,B,Q):
if C <=0: return 0
g = math.gcd(A,B)
if P*B <= Q*A:
    # S at least as efficient; enumerate y = number of T from 0 to A//g -1
    best = INF
    for y in range(A//g):
        rem = C - B*y
        if rem <=0:
            x=0
        else:
            x = (rem + A -1)//A
        cost = Q*y + P*x
        if cost < best: best=cost
    return best
else:
    # T more efficient; enumerate x = number of S from 0 to B//g -1
    best = INF
    for x in range(B//g):
        rem = C - A*x
        if rem <=0:
            y=0
        else:
            y = (rem + B -1)//B
        cost = P*x + Q*y
        if cost < best: best=cost
    return best

Let's maybe write code:

import sys, math

def min_cost(C, A, P, B, Q):
    if C <= 0:
        return 0
    g = math.gcd(A, B)
    if P * B <= Q * A:
        # S is not worse per lcm; enumerate T count y
        best = 10**30
        limit = A // g
        for y in range(limit):
            rem = C - B * y
            if rem <= 0:
                x = 0
            else:
                x = (rem + A - 1) // A
            cost = Q * y + P * x
            if cost < best:
                best = cost
        return best
    else:
        best = 10**30
        limit = B // g
        for x in range(limit):
            rem = C - A * x
            if rem <= 0:
                y = 0
            else:
                y = (rem + B - 1) // B
            cost = P * x + Q * y
            if cost < best:
                best = cost
        return best

def can(C, procs, X):
    total = 0
    for A,P,B,Q in procs:
        total += min_cost(C, A, P, B, Q)
        if total > X:
            return False
    return True

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]; X = data[1]
    procs = []
    idx=2
    for _ in range(N):
        A = data[idx]; P = data[idx+1]; B = data[idx+2]; Q = data[idx+3]
        procs.append((A,P,B,Q))
        idx += 4
    lo = 0
    hi = 100 * X + 1
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if can(mid, procs, X):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    print(lo)

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

def need(c, A, P, B, Q):
    g = gcd(A,B)
    if P*B <= Q*A:
        best = 10**30
        for y in range(A//g):
            rem = c - B*y
            if rem <= 0:
                x = 0
            else:
                x = (rem + A - 1)//A
            val = Q*y + P*x
            if val < best: best = val
        return best
...

Now, let's maybe derive a formal proof of min_cost to be confident:

For a fixed process, let g = gcd(A,B). The two bundles:
- `B/g` units of S produce `A*B/g` capacity and cost `P*B/g`.
- `A/g` units of T produce same capacity and cost `Q*A/g`.

If `P*B <= Q*A`, the S-bundle is no more expensive. In any solution with `y >= A/g`, replace `A/g` T's by `B/g` S's. Capacity unchanged, cost not increased. Repeating, there is an optimal solution with `0 <= y < A/g`. Then for each such y, the cheapest number of S's is `max(0, ceil((C - B*y)/A))`. Evaluate all y. Similarly if T-bundle is cheaper, bound x.

This proof is solid.

procs = []
for each:
    g = gcd(A,B)
    if P*B <= Q*A:
        limit = A//g
        mode = 0 # enumerate T
    else:
        limit = B//g
        mode = 1 # enumerate S
    procs.append((A,P,B,Q,limit,mode))

Now, let's maybe think about a possible counterexample to our min_cost formula due to "at least" and replacement with same capacity but maybe the replacement bundle uses more units of S and fewer T, but if we are enumerating y small, the x computed is minimal for that y. Suppose original optimal has y>=A/g and cost lower than any y<A/g after replacement? Replacement cost non-increase, so no. Good.

Let's prove cost non-increase in S efficient case:
Original cost = P x + Q y.
After replacing A/g T with B/g S:
x' = x + B/g, y' = y - A/g.
Cost' = P(x+B/g) + Q(y-A/g) = Px + Qy + (P*B/g - Q*A/g) = original + (P*B - Q*A)/g <= original. Good. Capacity: A(x+B/g)+B(y-A/g) = Ax + A*B/g + By - A*B/g = Ax+By. Good. So exact.

T efficient case:
Replace B/g S with A/g T:
x' = x - B/g, y' = y + A/g.
Cost' = P(x-B/g)+Q(y+A/g) = original + (Q*A - P*B)/g <0. Capacity same. Good.

Thus exact.

from math import gcd
def min_cost(c, A, P, B, Q):
    g = gcd(A, B)
    if P * B <= Q * A:
        best = 10**30
        for y in range(A // g):
            rem = c - B * y
            if rem <= 0:
                x = 0
            else:
                x = (rem + A - 1) // A
            cost = P * x + Q * y
            if cost < best:
                best = cost
        return best
    else:
        best = 10**30
        for x in range(B // g):
            rem = c - A * x
            if rem <= 0:
                y = 0
            else:
                y = (rem + B - 1) // B
            cost = P * x + Q * y
            if cost < best:
                best = cost
        return best

Note in S efficient branch, cost = P*x + Q*y. In T efficient branch, cost = P*x + Q*y. Same. Good.
